> **CLOSED — do not execute.** Closed 2026-09-13 with release 1.38.0 (C3.380 lifted the three runtimes to Artifactory-latest: Claude Agent SDK 0.3.251 / `claude` 2.1.251, codex rust-v0.154.0, Pi 0.84.4).
>
> **The honest figure, not the name.** The plan was named for 95 % and closes at the ledger's measured coverage on 632 rows counting decided refusals: **Pi 79 % · Claude 78 % · Codex 71 % · Host (Switch) 20 %** — `python3 scripts/plan-status.py docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-22-runtime-parity-closure.md` prints the live table (a reading with a date; the numbers here are the close-out's). The owner ruled on 2026-09-12 that 95 % is not to be chased: the remainder is `partial`/`absent`/`unknown` cells that are M/L builds, live measurements no rig has reached, or product postures.
>
> **What this plan did** (2026-09-11 → 2026-09-13, waves 15–19 + the rig day + the lifts): 137 owner questions answered over the ledger's gap families (C1/C2, the 1 043 ledger edits applied mechanically), 432 C3 build/refutation rows (550 tracker rows in all, 491 ✅), 12 owner postures decided under the owner's delegated rule (max parity, least code, what the SDK/binary ships), the C5 rig day on the packaged 1.37.0 build (four HIGH product defects found and fixed before the release), and the runtime lifts.
>
> **What is left, and where it went** (verdicts intact, nothing re-taken): every row still ⬜/🟨 at the close-out — 59 of them — was CARRIED — the auth/credential-shaped rows to `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-22-dp-auth-wave2-and-mcp-exposure.md` (as `DA2.*` rows), everything else to `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-integrations-and-authorization.md` (as `D1.*` rows); each source row below names its destination id. The queue order (owner 2026-09-13): the dp-auth plan first, the integrations plan last.

# Coverage-95 Parity Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development to execute this plan
> row by row — the controller pins rows (a controller-pins-rows deviation, as the parity closure did); lanes never
> edit this file. Rows are tracked in the `## Tracker` table below.

**Goal:** Take the capability ledger (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-28-capability-ledger.md`, 632 rows ×
Pi / Claude / Codex / Host) from 43 % / 37 % / 29 % coverage to 95–100 % *counting decided refusals* — every
cell either `covered`/`partial` with evidence, or `n/a` with the reason the owner gave on 2026-09-11 — by applying
the owner's 144 answers to the coverage-95 question round (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-11-coverage-95-owner-answers.md`,
§"Owner answers — 2026-09-11").

**Architecture:** three phases in the order the owner chose on 2026-09-11 — (1) re-stamp cells for landed work
(zero code: the ledger and the denominator sweep re-read against the tracker rows and commits they already carry),
(2) record every decided refusal as `n/a` with a verifiable reason, (3) build what the answers ask for — the S/M
rows and the five overrides (QH9 B, QK5 a toggle, QL4/QL8 by runtime capability, QL9 B + install on claude and
codex) — native-first, the host as the seam, one release (1.37.0) at the end. A fourth phase carries the flip
candidates: refusal recommendations the application pass found the runtime can do natively at ≤ S wiring, listed
for the owner instead of kept silently.

**Tech Stack:** Bun / TypeScript monorepo; three runtimes (Pi `@earendil-works/*` 0.84.3, Claude Agent SDK 0.3.247 /
Claude Code 2.1.247, codex rust-v0.150.1); the ledger tooling (`scripts/plan-status.py`, `scripts/check-ledger-citations.ts`,
`scripts/check-coverage-map.ts`); the CDP harness (`scripts/cdp/`).

## Global Constraints

- Owner rules in force: native-first with the host as the seam; read, do not invent; transparent and simple where
  the docs are silent; findings become plan rows at confirmation; a new row is inserted in sorted position
  (`python3 scripts/sort-plan-tracker.py <plan>`); commit in the atomic form `git commit -F <msg> -- <pathspec>`.
- Ledger edits: a verdict cell is one backticked word plus at most one marker (`ᵗ` tracker/landed commit, `ʰ` hand
  check, `ᵏ` code probe, `ˢ` propagated, `ᵈ` doc-only); an edit applies to the ledger AND its twin row in
  `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-05-denominator-gaps.md`; every `|` inside a cell is `\|`; read the row TOTAL
  (632) from `python3 scripts/plan-status.py docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-22-runtime-parity-closure.md` after every
  ledger commit; citations in the durable form (`` `path` symbol `Name` ``), never `path:line`; the citation
  gate `bun scripts/check-ledger-citations.ts` must not rise and only the controller lowers its pins.
- Codex evidence has two tiers: source (`github.com/openai/codex` at the shipped tag) or the shipped binary =
  strong; a page alone = intent. Claude: the page is the contract. Pi is built to Claude's pages.
- Coverage is READ, never asserted: the number quoted in any row is `plan-status.py`'s output on that day.
- The sandbox stays off; no MR, tag or publish during autonomous execution; no credential in any file.

## Pointers back

- Question round and answers: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-11-coverage-95-owner-questions.md` (evidence),
  `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-11-coverage-95-owner-answers.md` (questions, recommendations, the owner's answers).
- The plan this one follows: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-22-runtime-parity-closure.md` (closed at 1.36.0).
- The plan that follows this one: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-integrations-and-authorization.md`.

## Application rules — what the sixteen readers found while writing the edits (read before applying ANY family)

The edits are machine-readable in `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-11-coverage-95-apply/<F>.json` (items: qid, answer,
kind, ledger_edits[row, column, from, to, why_en], plan_rows, flip_candidate); each family's `<F>-readme.json` carries
the reader's own caveats verbatim. Cross-cutting rules distilled from them:

1. **Two files, two vocabularies.** The capability ledger writes `` `covered ᵗ` `` (a backticked word plus at most one
   marker); the denominator sweep `2026-09-05-denominator-gaps.md` writes marker-less `have` / `partial` / `absent` /
   `n/a` / `unknown` and a different column layout. Translate a ledger `covered` to the twin's `have`; never paste a
   ledger cell into the twin verbatim. Family J's twin ids differ from the ledger's (the ledger's J60–J64 are the twin's
   J12–J16): apply by the `twin_row` field where the item carries one, never by id alone.
2. **Rows without a twin.** Rows that predate the P6.189 admission (A1/A9/A10, K3/K5/K6/K7, L2/L4/L6/L7/L10/L11/L15/L16,
   N2/N3, O2/O5/O6, …) exist in the ledger only; an edit to them touches one file.
3. **A `from` that no longer matches HEAD is a finding, not a skip** — re-read the row, then either re-derive the edit
   or record why the reader's premise moved.
4. **Why-only edits.** Where `from == to` the edit changes the Why cell (or the reason word `DECISION`/`PLANNED`) and not
   the verdict; the ledger has no separate Switch column in the schema, so Switch re-stamps ride in the plan-row notes.
5. **Refusals wear `n/a ᵗ`** — an owner answer recorded in the answer sheet and carried as a row here is tracker-level
   evidence (`ᵈ` is reserved for vendor-page verdicts). Family O deliberately keeps `absent` + a DECISION reason where
   the reader judged `n/a` would subtract a capability from the denominator the owner meant to keep visible.
6. **Contingent stamps** (a cell whose evidence is a row of THIS plan that has not landed) are carried in the plan-row
   note, not in `ledger_edits` — apply them when the named row lands, never before.
7. **Family X overlaps other families** (QX1↔K/M9, QX2↔N/N6/N10, QX4↔A/A2/A7/A64): merge the Why sentences, do not
   write two.
8. **Row TOTAL and gates after every commit:** `python3 scripts/plan-status.py <parity plan>` must read 632 ledger rows
   before and after; `bun scripts/check-ledger-citations.ts` exit 0 with no pin raised; every citation in the durable
   form (`` `path` symbol `Name` ``).

| C3.391 | `dp auth print-token` runs under a 5 s budget (`PRINT_TOKEN_TIMEOUT_MS`) that races dp's OWN startup hook — nessy-cli measured the hook opening a network connection and giving up on its own after ~5 s when its host is unreachable, so a 5 s budget aborted dp right before it printed the token (their AIDT-7828 raised it to 30 s); measure our arm with the host unreachable and size the budget against the hook, not against the token | ✅ | `aa29bc107` | `[host]` `[pi]` Born 2026-09-12 from the controller's read of the nessy-cli delta (nessy-cli commit `3e90c2fd7`, `packages/core/src/dp/dpConfig.ts` `printTokenTimeout: 30_000`, the measured reason in its comment: any dp SUBCOMMAND runs the startup hook, the `--version`/`--help` flags do not). Ours: `packages/shared/src/auth/dp/dp-api.ts` `PRINT_TOKEN_TIMEOUT_MS = 5_000` (identical budget, identical subcommand, the same shipped binary — dp 13.50.0 on this rig); `dp-binary.ts` `VERSION_TIMEOUT_MS = 5_000` is NOT affected by their reading. XS–S; wave 19, lane W19-a (`auth/dp/**`). Native-first: a reading of the shipped dp on this machine decides the budget (`time dp auth print-token` with the corp host unreachable — a refusing local proxy or an `HTTPS_PROXY` pointed at a closed loopback port, never a real network change on the rig; quote the wall time); the broker's transient-vs-durable rule stands (a Timeout must not pin `needs-login`). Done means: the budget is set against the MEASURED hook time with the reading quoted at the constant; one test that a slow-but-successful print-token inside the budget is a token, not a Timeout; the C3.381 notice names a timeout distinctly from a sign-out. **CORRECTION 2026-09-12 21:30 (controller, from the dp-auth audit's skeptic pass — confirmed at HEAD):** the row's exclusion of `VERSION_TIMEOUT_MS` is FALSE. nessy-cli's comment exempts only the `--version`/`--help` FLAGS from the startup hook, and our `dp-binary.ts` probe runs `dp version` — a SUBCOMMAND — so it races the same hook under the same 5 s; and `session-broker.ts` symbol `runProbe` awaits `getDpVersion` BEFORE `printToken`, sequentially, so with the host unreachable the version probe can spend its whole budget first and the print-token never even starts inside one probe window. Measure BOTH arms in the same sitting (`time dp version` vs `time dp --version` vs `time dp auth print-token`, host unreachable), and size both budgets against the hook; the transient-vs-durable rule of the broker stands for both. When the budget is raised, raise the docblock sentence beside the constant with it (`dp-api.ts` still says 'Timeout (5s)'). **W19-a 2026-09-12:** both dp budgets (`dp version`, `dp auth print-token`) sized against dp's own startup hook and the two probes made concurrent instead of additive; the row's exclusion of `VERSION_TIMEOUT_MS` REFUTED and measured. |

| C4.1 | FLIP CANDIDATE QA4 (family A): the owner's answer keeps a refusal where the runtime has the mechanism natively and the wiring is small | ✅ | — | `[product]` OWNER: (A) keep the refusal as answered on 2026-09-11 — default if unanswered; (B) wire it. The refused half has a native mechanism and small wiring: codex's own `turn/start` accepts `sandboxPolicy` (`packages/codex-protocol/generated/v2/TurnStartParams.ts`, documented as overriding for this turn and subsequent turns) and we ALREADY send `approvalPolicy` on that same call since P6.223, so adding the field is S. It is refused for a measured reason - a second source of truth for the sandbox, where a wrong `networkAccess` is a silent widening - so the flip is the owner's to make, with the mode table kept as the single writer. **OWNER 2026-09-11: A — accepted the recommendation (keep).** Механизм подтверждён в дереве дословно, но паритета он не добавляет: песочная половина уже едет на следующем ходу через re-fork (пинится тестом), а остаток, из-за которого ячейка `partial` — роли из `config.toml` — `sandboxPolicy` не закрывает, так что по критерию «ближе к паритету, меньше кода» это чистый прирост кода при нулевом приросте покрытия. Вдобавок это продуктовая сделка по безопасности: второй писатель прав, где ошибка в `networkAccess` — тихое расширение, причём `networkAccess` несёт и `readOnly`, так что вариант C от этого вектора не свободен. |

| C5.21 | CRU-7: the WebFetch domain preflight leaks hostnames to api.anthropic.com and nobody has driven it | ✅ | — | `[live]` Carried from runtime-parity-closure P3.352 (verdicts intact, glyph there was 🟨) at the 1.36.0 close-out, 2026-09-11 — the release sitting (two passes on the packaged build, `docs/testing/cdp-runs/2026-09-11-release-sitting-1.36.0.md`) could not drive CRU-7 to a verdict: `[sweep:silent-control]` **Coverage sweep 2026-09-04 (owner directive: nothing forgotten), verified against HEAD by an adversarial verifier; lane A — claude runtime & switch costs; size medium; runtimes claude-runtime, pi, codex.** The checklist and `claude-agent.ts` both say the egress "cannot be proven from this tree" — false: the shipped 2.1.233 binary is greppable and states it. The call site is `if(!ka().skipWebFetchPreflight) switch((await J4d(b,n)).status){case"blocked":throw…}`, the endpoint is a HARDCODED `https://api.anthropic.com/api/web/domain_info?domain=…` (so the loopback adapter cannot intercept it, and the hostname travels as a query parameter), the cache is allow-only, 5-minute, 128-entry, and the setting's own describe names enterprise environments. The page agrees and says the check runs regardless of provider and is not affected by `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC`. What is genuinely live-only shrinks to: does this execute in a packaged gateway session, and is `WebFetch` in that tool set. Detector must use a FRESH hostname per run (a cached one produces no egress and reads exactly like a fix), read the proxy log rather than the model's reply, carry the actual WebFetch GET as the positive control, and use the cheap discriminator — block api.anthropic.com at the proxy: fresh hostname must FAIL, cached must SUCCEED.
